\displaystyle-2\,\frac{GM}{c^{2}}\,\frac{d^{i}_{\sigma}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{x_{\rm N}}\right)+2\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\sigma^{a}\sigma^{b}\sigma^{i}-\sigma^{a}\delta^{bi}}{\left(x_{\rm N}\right)^{3}}+6\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\sigma^{a}d_{\sigma}^{b}d_{\sigma}^{i}}{\left(x_{\rm N}\right)^{5}}

(336)

\displaystyle+8\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}\sigma^{a}\sigma^{b}d_{\sigma}^{i}-d_{\sigma}^{a}d_{\sigma}^{b}d_{\sigma}^{i}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{4}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{x_{\rm N}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{1}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{\left(x_{\rm N}\right)^{3}}+\frac{3}{8}\,\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{\left(x_{\rm N}\right)^{5}}\right]

\displaystyle+2\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{2\,d_{\sigma}^{a}\delta^{bi}-5\,\sigma^{a}\sigma^{b}d_{\sigma}^{i}-2\,\sigma^{a}d_{\sigma}^{b}\sigma^{i}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{x_{\rm N}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{\left(x_{\rm N}\right)^{3}}\right],

where the impact vector is given by (
33
), that means

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\times\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\right)\quad{\rm and}\quad d_{\sigma}=\left|\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}\right|.

(337)

The expression
\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 1PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\right)
in (
123
) is obtained from (
336
) by replacing the argument

\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}
by
\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}
everywhere. That means

\displaystyle n^{i}_{\rm 1PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\,\frac{GM}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\hat{d}^{i}_{\sigma}}{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{x_{\rm 1PN}}\right)+2\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\sigma^{a}\sigma^{b}\sigma^{i}-\sigma^{a}\delta^{bi}}{\left(x_{\rm 1PN}\right)^{3}}+6\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\sigma^{a}\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{b}\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{i}}{\left(x_{\rm 1PN}\right)^{5}}

(338)

\displaystyle+8\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{2}\sigma^{a}\sigma^{b}\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{i}-\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{a}\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{b}\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{i}}{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{4}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{x_{\rm 1PN}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{1}{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{\left(x_{\rm 1PN}\right)^{3}}+\frac{3}{8}\,\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{\left(x_{\rm 1PN}\right)^{5}}\right]

\displaystyle+2\,\frac{G\hat{M}_{ab}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{2\,\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{a}\delta^{bi}-5\,\sigma^{a}\sigma^{b}\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{i}-2\,\sigma^{a}\hat{d}_{\sigma}^{b}\sigma^{i}}{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{x_{\rm 1PN}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{\left(x_{\rm 1PN}\right)^{3}}\right],

where the new impact vector

\displaystyle\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\times\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\right)

(339)

has been introduced; note that
\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}}=0
and its absolute value
\hat{d}_{\sigma}=\left|\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}_{\sigma}}\right|
.
In view of Eq. (
64
) the new impact vector (
339
) is determined by

\displaystyle\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\left(\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\times\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\right)

(340)

where
\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}
is given by Eq.(
65
) with (
66
) and (
67
).
For the absolute value of (
340
) and the inverse powers one obtains in 1PN approximation

\displaystyle\hat{d_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d_{\sigma}+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}\cdot\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{d_{\sigma}}\;,

(341)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(\hat{d}_{\sigma}\right)^{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{n}}-\frac{n}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{n}}\,\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}\cdot\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\;.

(342)

The relation (
342
) is actually the first term of a series expansion where the higher order terms are beyond 1PN approximation. This series
expansion has a convergence radius determined by the condition
n\,\left|\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}\cdot\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\right|\leq\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}
.
Inserting the monopole term one finds
4\,n\,m\,x_{1}<\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}
; note that the inverse powers
2\leq n\leq 6
according to
Eq. (
338
). Using the extreme case of Jupiter or even the Sun one finds that this condition
is well satisfied for any observer located in the Solar System, and is spoiled for observers if they would have distances of more than several hundred

{\rm au}
from the massive Solar System body.

In order to determine the expression
\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}
in Eq. (
124
),

\displaystyle\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\right)=\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 1PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\right)-\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 1PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\right)+{\cal O}\left(c^{-6}\right),

(343)

where
\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 1PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\right)
and
\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 1PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\right)
are given by Eqs. (
336
) and
(
338
), respectively, one has to perform a series expansion of
\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 1PN}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm 1PN}\right)
up to terms of the
order
{\cal O}\left(c^{-6}\right)
to be consistent with the 2PN approximation. In this way, by means of relations (
227
) - (
229
)
as well as (
340
) - (
342
), one obtains

\displaystyle\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}+\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}+\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}\;,

(344)

with

\displaystyle\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\frac{G^{2}M^{2}}{c^{4}}\,\frac{1}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{x_{\rm N}}\right)\frac{x_{\rm N}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}-x_{0}-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{0}}{d_{\sigma}}\;,

(345)

and

\displaystyle\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\,\frac{G^{2}M^{2}}{c^{4}}\,J_{2}\,\frac{P^{2}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{4}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{x_{\rm N}}\right)\frac{x_{\rm N}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}-x_{0}-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{0}}{d_{\sigma}}

(346)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\bigg(1-\left(\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$e$}_{3}\right)^{2}-3\,\left(\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$e$}_{3}}{d_{\sigma}}\right)^{2}\bigg)\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}}{d_{\sigma}}+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$e$}_{3}}{d_{\sigma}}\;\mbox{\boldmath$e$}_{3}\Bigg],

and

\displaystyle\Delta\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}\left(\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 12\,\frac{G^{2}M^{2}}{c^{4}}\,\left(J_{2}\right)^{2}\,\frac{P^{4}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{6}}\left(1+\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}}{x_{\rm N}}\right)\frac{x_{\rm N}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}-x_{0}-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{0}}{d_{\sigma}}\left(1-\left(\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$e$}_{3}\right)^{2}\right)^{2}\,\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}}{d_{\sigma}}\;,

(347)

where in (
346
) the quadrupole tensor (
305
) and in (
347
) the product
of two quadrupole tensors (
330
) have been inserted.
In (
345
) - (
347
) all terms which are proportional to
\frac{d_{\sigma}}{x_{0}}
and

\frac{d_{\sigma}}{x_{\rm N}}
have been omitted, because they contribute less than
1\,{\rm nas}
even for grazing light rays at any massive Solar System body
and can be neglected even for high-precision astrometry on the sub-
\mu
as
and nas-level of accuracy. The calculation of (
344
) has
revealed that the
enhanced 2PN terms
originate from relations (
340
) - (
342
), that means they are solely caused by the
new impact vector
\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$d$}_{\sigma}}
in (
339
).

In (
345
) - (
347
) one can replace the unperturbed position of the light signal
at time of reception,
\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\left(t_{1}\right)
, by the exact position of the observer,
\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{1}
, in view of (
122
),
that means such a replacement in (
345
) - (
347
) would cause an error of the
order
{\cal O}\left(c^{-6}\right)
which is in line with the 2PN approximation.
In this way one finally arrives at the expression as given by Eqs. (
133
) - (
135
).
By evaluating the absolute value of the light deflection
\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}\right|
one finds the upper limits of
these
enhanced terms
as given by Eqs. (
144
) - (
146
).

Appendix K
Proof of relations (
141
) - (
143
)

Finally we will proof the upper limits given in relations (
141
) - (
143
). The expression for
\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}
is
formally given by Eq. (
121
), and the light deflection of this term reads

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}_{\rm 2PN}}{c}-\left(\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}_{\rm 1PN}}{c}\right)\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}_{\rm 1PN}}{c}\;.

(348)

By inspection of Eqs. (
81
) and (
82
) one obtains three terms

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}_{\rm 2PN}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}\;,

(349)

with

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}_{\rm 2PN}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}_{\rm 2PN}}{c}-\bigg(\!\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\rm M}_{\rm 1PN}}{c}\!\bigg)\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\rm M}_{\rm 1PN}}{c}\,,

(350)

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}}{c}-2\bigg(\!\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\rm Q}_{\rm 1PN}}{c}\!\bigg)\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\rm M}_{\rm 1PN}}{c},

(351)

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}}{c}-\bigg(\!\frac{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\rm Q}_{\rm 1PN}}{c}\!\bigg)\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\frac{\Delta\dot{\mbox{\boldmath$x$}}^{\rm Q}_{\rm 1PN}}{c}\,,

(352)

where the argument
\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{\rm N}\left(t_{1}\right)
can be replaced by the exact spatial position of the observer
\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{1}
in view of (
122
).
The absolute value of (
349
) can be estimated by

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}_{\rm 2PN}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|+\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|+\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|\;.

(353)

By inserting Eqs. (
62
) - (
63
) and
Eqs. (
83
) - (
85
) into (
350
) - (
352
) one might
determine these upper limits in (
353
). Such an ambitious calculation is, however, not necessary, because of the following relations:

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$\nu$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm M}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|\leq\frac{G^{2}M^{2}}{c^{4}}\left|\frac{15}{4}\frac{\pi}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}-8\frac{x_{0}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{0}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{3}}\right|,

(354)

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$\nu$}^{{\rm M}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|\leq 4\,\frac{G^{2}M^{2}}{c^{4}}\,\left|J_{2}\right|\,\frac{P^{2}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\,\left|\frac{15}{4}\,\frac{\pi}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}+8\,\frac{x_{0}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{0}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{3}}\right|,

(355)

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$n$}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\times\mbox{\boldmath$\nu$}^{{\rm Q}\times{\rm Q}}_{\rm 2PN}\right|\leq 2\,\frac{G^{2}M^{2}}{c^{4}}\,\left|J_{2}\right|^{2}\,\frac{P^{4}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{4}}\,\left|\frac{15}{4}\,\frac{\pi}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{2}}+12\,\frac{x_{0}+\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$x$}_{0}}{\left(d_{\sigma}\right)^{3}}\right|,

(356)

where we have used the upper limits of the 2PN terms in the total light deflection as given by relations (
108
) - (
110
).
In view of relation (
136
), these expressions coincide with the asserted relations in (
141
) - (
143
).
